ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u11	Thank you. I would like to remind you that the Sejm has agreed that in the discussion on this item it will hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Arkadiusz Czartoryski will be the first to speak on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u12	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Ladies and gentlemen! The draft law amending the Law on the State Protection Service, which is the subject of today's work, essentially addresses two issues. The first refers to a change in the way in which the allowance limit is set for the remuneration of State Protection Officers. This amendment is designed to make the system of allowances more flexible, which are one of the elements of the officers' salary system, by setting a total limit on the amount of allowances. At present, the limitation for an allowance justified by specific qualifications, conditions or place of service is 20% the basic salary, in turn the maximum amount of the special allowance shall be 50% of that salary. In the amendment of the Act, it is proposed to set a total limit of 75% of the two additives mentioned. basic salary. Ladies and gentlemen, we believe that this will help to shape the remuneration of the State Protection Service officers in a manner that is appropriate to the tasks and competences they carry out. Another issue that the amendment deals with is the financial situation of the State Protection Service officers and the extension of the period for which the special allowance is fixed, from the current 6 months to the year. In addition, the draft law explicitly defines the situation of officials only admitted or transferred to the State Security Service from other services. In their case, the special allowance would be granted for the first time from the start of the service in the State Protection Service until the nearest 31 July and thereafter according to the general rule for each subsequent year. Ladies and gentlemen! Another issue that is regulated in the draft law is Article. The 390th Law on the State Protection Service, which takes into account the increase in the upper limits of the financial resources allocated to the operation of the State Protection Service, since the current amount determined for a period of 10 years, was calculated according to indicators in force in 2017. The provisions of the Article. 2 projects are adapted: they adapt the current system of additives to the changes made. A special allowance is foreseen for a transitional period from 1 September 2018 to 31 July 2019 to ensure a peaceful and undistorted transition to the target system. The project provides for the fixing of allowances only in the event of changes in their amount. Ladies and gentlemen! The State Protection Service, in a situation in which its earnings and financial conditions are not the highest in today's labour market, should be able to flexibly shape the service allowances so as to take account of the transfer of an officer, often several hundred kilometres from the place of residence, and to direct him to carry out his duties, as well as to be able to pay attention to the special qualifications acquired from the State Protection Service officers, often very well-educated young people, and thus to form allowances, so that appropriate incentives give rise to the decision to work in the State Protection Service, hence the Law and Justice Club fully supports the bill in question. Thank you very much. [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u13	Thank you very much. Mr Marek Wójcik, the Civic Platform Club, will speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u14	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the draft law amending the Law on State Protection Services. The amendment concerns two issues. The first is a change in the method of calculating the allowance, and the second is an increase in the limit on the expenditure of the State budget for the State Protection Service in the next 10 years. Protection of the government will be even more expensive, because the law provides for an increase in expenditure on the State Protection Service by PLN 330 million over the next 10 years. Of course, there will be years in which these expenditures will be even more increased, e.g. 2019 will be the year in which the SOP budget will increase by as much as 20%. I would like to remind you that the law, which introduced this expenditure rule and provided for some kind of estimate of expenditure, was adopted by the Sejm only six months ago, because the SOP Act came from December 2017, and therefore the increase of the budget of the State Security Service today just six months after the adoption of the previous law indicates that something did not work out for you, that that law was simply underdeveloped. It is worth recalling the announcements made by you in that debate. Then, when preparing the SOP Act, then Minister Mariusz Błaszczak and Deputy Minister Jarosław Zieliński spoke about the fact that the new formation will receive new competences, will carry out operational and cognitive activities, will protect more facilities, that more officers will be employed in it. At that time, we warned in the debate that in a situation where all the services have a clear view of human resources problems, such changes, the decommissioning of the BOR and the equiping of the new service with additional competences would simply end in disaster. Unfortunately, this is because today in the State Security Service we have problems with the wave of leaving officers, problems with recruitment. As far as the protection of the objects that you spoke of in this debate is concerned, the protection of government buildings in Warsaw has been transferred to the police. The real symbol of this law is that until now the Office of Government Protection has been responsible for protecting the premises of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, and now the police being distracted from other tasks are responsible for the protection. This shows that the implementation of the assumptions that you have adopted by creating the SOP law has simply failed. Unfortunately, the answer to this situation is cannibalization of other services. The policy of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration leads to an unprecedented distinction between the powers of police officers and the status of police officers, Border Guards, State Fire Department and SOP officers. Let me remind you that in June you announced a new regulation that had already raised the salaries of SOP officers. Today, these allowances will be a kind of increase, but above all the way in which these allowances are calculated will certainly be better for SOP officers than it has been so far. In our opinion, it's designed to attract officers to the SOP from other uniforms. Minister, it really is a problem, if from small cells - which are dealing with e.g. two persons shall be taken to work with a number of officers. That's the problem, because these officers have no one to replace them with. Border Guard officers or police officers are responsible for universal security. Those officers transferred to the SOP will only be responsible for the government's security. It's not right. In addition, it is not only a weakening of universal security, but it is unfortunately the division of officers on the worse and better - the worse ones, i.e. those who work in other services, the better ones, that is, those who protect the government. There would be nothing wrong with raising wages, but, please, think of all the services, just as it has been all these years, when all the uniformed services under the Ministry of Foreign Affairs have been treated in the same way. I would like to draw attention to one more thing, i.e. for the trial of this law, because it entered the Sejm on 28 June. Let me remind you that the police protest began earlier on June 26th. In my opinion, proceding a law that increases the budget of only one formation, but completely does not refer to the difficult situation of the officers of the other formations supervised by the Minister of Home Affairs, in the situation of the ongoing protest of uniformed services, is either a provocation or a complete disregard of the social partners. I am convinced, Minister, that sooner or later you will pay for such arrogance, complete disregard for the social partners and pressing this law now by the Sejm day by day during the ongoing protest of the other services. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u16	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I would like to present a brief - because the law is quite short - a statement of the Kukiz club15 concerning the amendment of the Law on the State Protection Service. This is a bill that actually, as it was here mentioned, not so long ago we accepted in the Seym with various reservations, also at a rather rapid pace. With reservations that in a moment we will need a lot of amendments in connection with it, for example because of the adoption of a whole series or a package of other laws. But this bill was passed. Indeed, this amendment, as has already been emphasized here twice, brings financial benefits to those working in the State Protection Service, in the new creation, because it actually makes the system of additives one of the elements of the civil servants' salary system more flexible and provides greater financial stability for the State Protection Service officers by introducing an annual deadline in place of the current six-month period. These are certainly changes received positively by this service and by its servants. But our role is also to think about how it's done and why it's done right now. Because, indeed, it is impossible to disagree that if we adopted a large law that created a new service in place of the Office for Government Protection, it would be necessary to foresee costs, all the more so as to mention it, and in committee, and in the plenary room it was said that this change would generate certain costs. These costs were clearly underestimated. They may have been underestimated because no one thought it would be necessary to encourage people to come to this new service in some way. And now half of poverty, if this financial incentive were to draw new people from the outside, feeding this service to new people, training them and building them. On the other hand, the problem is that it seems that we may have to deal with such a solution by underappreciating or unequal treatment of other services will result in the transition from those services to the State Protection Service, which would be clearly unfavorable. I think that also Law and Justice and the Minister understand that such a trend would be undesirable and dangerous. So the question is: How will this be secured, and is the government even thinking about securing this kind of flow at the moment, so that we don't have a personal deficit in other services, because there will be worse conditions? That's one thing. Secondly, it nevertheless generates costs on the part of the state budget. I am, as am my group, such a terrible literal supporter of the sustainable budget. Here I am different from Prime Minister Morawiecki, and probably from most of you, probably from most Polish politicians, who are rather accustomed to budget based on deficit. So right now, if you want to spend that kind of money, I'm interested in where it's coming from. Is it the addition of money that will appear as a form of budget deficit, or is it the reduction of money that may be spent on other services or other activities of the Ministry of Interior and Administration, or is it really a rational transfer of some money from such spheres in which the State may not be involved at the moment and in which these expenses could be cut? And that is also a very important answer for us. Of course, we strongly support that those who are involved in protecting Poles in general, but also the highest government officials, pay decent money, so that they want to come to this service, so that they can serve in the so-called. holy peace, they did not think whether they were enough for the first or not, because who like who, but us, Poles, should know that we should not save for the protection of the highest government officials, because it always ends very badly and tragically. But we are interested in answers to these specific questions - and questions about financial issues, and questions about possible, possibly negative, consequences associated with the movement of people from other formations, for example from the police. Because indeed the problem of protest and the doubts of the police mom. For example, the bill the Senate filed on police pensions is still in the freezer and is not considered by the Sejm. Here, too, the question is why we are not dealing with this law at all, which was a law desirable by the police and which is currently in the Seym, even a print number is given. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u18	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Modern Club in relation to the report of the committee on the draft law amending the Law on State Protection Services, print No. 2692. There is a repeat of a situation where, in a rather short time after the introduction of the main law, we need to change it and we need to amend it. It has already been noted here that, indeed, these financial considerations, which were not included in the main law, should be there and it would not have to be amended. Nevertheless, it must be acknowledged that the State does not spoil the State Protection Service officers. These salaries do not encourage you to join the service. We know that there's staff problems as well. In general, the situation of the officers of both the State Security Service and the police - and these have recently taken up protest action - is unbelievable. The budget situation is what it is, but it must be taken into account that these officers are responsible for our security and if we want high-level officers and morale and those who want to devote themselves to this service should be well rewarded. We therefore have no objections. as to the fact that certain new rules are being laid down. Perhaps it would be better to set a higher wage for everyone, not just in the form of extras. I just hope... The biggest reservations we have are that there will be very much discretion when it comes to granting these additives. We don't know what the grounds for granting such an allowance will be for a good cause. The range is also huge, because from 20% to 74% basic salary. This is actually quite a discrepancy. And the question is this: Who will be responsible for this, and who will eventually admit it, determine the height of these allowances? I hope that, above all, we will take into account the threat to the performance of the function. Otherwise, I think we can judge an officer of the State Security Service who rides with one of the ministers, or the one who protects the Polish diplomatic institution in Kabul, for example. It is known that these threats are completely different, and therefore it seems that allowances for those who put their health at a much higher rate, sometimes life... The point is to make them pay better. It also raises controversy, and the question arises as to whether other servants will not feel a little offended by the fact that one service has been chosen, which will have a higher salary. What about the others? We are in favour of adopting this bill. We have some doubts, but I hope that the rules, which are likely to follow and determine exactly what these allowances will be for, will indeed be well prepared and clear conditions on the basis of which such an officer will receive higher allowances. In general, we also have a question that came up here. Namely, as regards those funds which are earmarked for the State Protection Service of more than PLN 3 billion over the next 10 years, there is no clarification as to what percentage of these funds will be earmarked for remuneration. That hasn't been clarified either. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u50	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The Commission at its meeting on 2 July this year. carried out the first reading and considered the same bill. The project aims to adapt the field structure of inland waterway administration to carry out the new tasks imposed by the Water Law Act and concerns the transformation of inland waterway authorities in Gdańsk, Giżycko, Warsaw, Krakow and Kędzierzyn-Koźl in the delegation. The Water Law Act, which entered into force on 1 January 2018, provides for the imposition of new obligations on the directors of inland waterway transport offices, inter alia. on the construction, reconstruction and modernisation of inland waterways of particular transport importance, evaluation and reconciliation of planning and investment documents. The current institutional structure of the inland waterway administration will not ensure active policies in the field of inland waterway transport and promote the development of this mode of transport. In the context of the new obligations imposed on IWT's directors and with a view to sound management of public funds, it is necessary to reorganise the authorities and therefore the proposal involves organisational changes aimed at strengthening IWT's offices. It also envisages adapting them to new tasks while ensuring adequate access for citizens to public services provided by public authorities and the proper implementation of statutory tasks. The Commission adopted the drafting of an editorial and legislative amendment that had been tabled. Without opposition, an amendment was adopted to introduce a provision concerning the fact that entries made before the date of entry into force of this Act in registers and records and changes in registers and records made by the directors of inland waterway transport offices in Gdańsk, Giżycko, Warsaw, Krakow and Kędzierzyna-Koźlu remain valid. The Commission accepted without objection the draft law report and I ask the High House to adopt the committee report in print No 2679. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u51	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. The first vote will be taken by Mr Jerzy Materna, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u52	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present a position towards the government's draft law amending the IWT Act, print no. 2653 and 2679. There are two main reasons for amending the IWT Act. First, it is the entry into force of the Water Law Act on 1 January 2018, which provides for the imposition of new obligations on the directors of inland waterway transport offices, inter alia. as regards the construction, reconstruction and modernisation of inland waterways of particular transport importance, as well as the evaluation and reconciliation of planning and investment documents. The current institutional structure would not ensure active policies in the field of inland waterway transport and promote the development of this mode of transport. Secondly, the entry into force of Regulation (EU) 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals imposes on administrative authorities, including the directors of inland waterways, the need to ensure adequate standards for the protection and processing of personal data, which entails an increased number of tasks of those bodies. In the current state of inland waterway transport offices, as is apparent from the Supreme Audit Chamber report, are underinvested. Insufficient employment and relatively low wages in relation to the necessary knowledge, qualifications and experience requirements of workers make it difficult to carry out the statutory tasks of IWT directors. In the current structure, financial support was mentioned above. the Offices shall be provided by the Chief Accounting Officer of the Office serving the Minister responsible for inland waterway transport. In addition, one person is responsible for administrative and financial matters and for the inland waterway authorities in most offices. In particular offices, as a rule, there are no persons employed in independent specialist positions, e.g. for defence, legal, classified information, protection of personal data, IT, public procurement or organizational and technical matters. In the context of the new obligations imposed on IWT directors, this proposal envisages organisational changes aimed at strengthening IWT offices and adapting them to new tasks while ensuring adequate access for citizens to public services provided by public service authorities and the correct implementation of statutory tasks. The amendment of the law introduces a reform of inland waterway authorities. The number of central offices is reduced from the present eight to three. These are offices in Bydgoszcz, Wrocław and Szczecin. The remaining existing offices in Kędzierzyn-Koźlu, Krakow, Warsaw, Gdańsk and Giżycko will constitute delegations. Another change leads to full competence in the three main offices, i.e. as regards financial and accounting matters, for investments on inland waterways of particular transport importance. The number of administrative and financial, organisational and technical and personnel matters will increase in three IWT offices. For the above reasons, which I have mentioned, it is planned to hire 30 people for inland waterway authorities. The draft law is compatible with European Union law. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the government project and will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Therefore, this sector needs to strengthen the expertise so that there is no situation in the State Water Farm of Poland, where the lack of consequences in operation, resulting in the drain of professional personnel, delays even several months in the issue of water permits and water-related documents, not only inhibiting the investments related to water, but even eliminating them. However, hoping that the spirit of the IWT Act will flow other than the Act on the establishment of the state farm of Polish Waters, we are aware of the great amount of work to be done on waterways and waterways, therefore we ask that shipping offices in Krakow and Warsaw be left behind, so that inland transport and shipping on the Vistula River can also be reborn alongside the international Odrzańska Waterway. How is this regulated in other countries of the European Union? Because there are objections to why we are introducing such regulations. High Izbo, in the 20 countries of the European Union, these electoral districts are single, i.e. There is one electoral district for each country, and of course the principle of proportionality, which is set by the EU directive on elections to the European Parliament, applies. But interestingly, in these larger countries, the other seven, because I no longer count the UK - but I would like to stress that in the UK these mandate districts were also multi-faceted, also starting with at least three mandates - in countries such as Italy, Spain, Ireland, France, and the Netherlands, there are multi-faceted districts, with at least three seats bigger - even four. In Italy, the largest electoral district is 17-mandate. So we think that the allegations that we are using one or another variable in these elections, since this is acceptable and this is how it is done in many countries, are pointless. This legal status favoured electoral districts with a large number of people entitled to vote and a large number of electoral turnout, so it was called. A wandering voice. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u122	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the joint committees, I present a report by the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy on the Government's draft law on the conversion of the right to use perpetual land built for housing purposes into land ownership rights, print Nos 2673 and 2729, namely the printing in which the report is included. The bill removes legal barriers. Ladies and gentlemen, we are dealing with a very diverse situation of the owners of the premises, as some of them have already acquired ownership of the land occupied, but some are vainly seeking the exercise of their right. In the light of the above, the government project envisages converting the right to use perpetual land built for housing purposes into property rights by law within a certain period of time, i.e. 1 January 2019 Such a solution means that all members of the housing communities are subject to a statutory transformation without the requirement to allow for a change of law. The proposed law also covers owners of single-family homes. According to the Government's proposal, the authorisation of perpetual users will be paid for, but it is, of course, envisaged to apply reductions in conversion fees in the form of discounts. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law was examined on 12 July 2018 at a joint meeting of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, which took place at the seat of the Ministry of Investment and Development at ul. Common in Warsaw. The meeting was also attended by representatives of the Association of Polish Cities and a nationwide association agreement to defend the rights of members of housing cooperatives. Ladies and gentlemen, the Act transforms the perpetual use of land with housing into the property right of the land in an arbitrary manner, because by law with a statutory date. Ladies and gentlemen, the bill is very expected. On behalf of the joint committees, I would like to inform the High House that a large number of drafting and legislative amendments were tabled during the meeting. There were no substantive amendments. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law contains these amendments, the print report 2729 contains these amendments. During the discussions with the public, questions were raised, of course answers were also presented. Consequently, the draft law contained in this printing contains 29 articles. Substantive provisions are added in Article. 5, in Article 5, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 6, in Article 2, the following paragraph is added: 16 and, as I said, many legislative and editorial amendments. It is clear that many Poles will benefit from this bill. On the other hand, local authorities will lose their future income, i.e. residents at the end. The Act states that each Pole who is a user of the perpetual land on which he owns an apartment or a house will be the owner of this land by law, after payment of the conversion fee. The Act gives the possibility to pay this fee for the next 20 years or in full, in advance. In the case of perpetual use on the State Treasury land, the situation is clear, because for payment of the fee in full only in the first year after conversion each will receive 60% Increases. The problem arises in the case of perpetual use on the ground of a local government unit. Revenue from perpetual land use charges is one of the revenues of the local government's own units. This law removes one of the sources of funding for the tasks of local governments. The responsible government should propose to cover financial losses in the form of compensation, not, like the current one, to shift further tasks to local governments without providing them with the means to do so. Next year - 2019 these losses will not yet be present, so we will propose, as the Civic Platform in the Budget Act for 2020 that compensates local governments for them. Another strange solution to this law is the introduction of the highest discounts for the payment of the conversion fee only in the first year after the conversion of perpetual use from the State Treasury. I stress: only from the Treasury. And the first year after the transformation is the 2019 election year. You've introduced a 60% incentive. discounts only in the first year, so that Poles will pay the entire fee as soon as possible in 2019 when you are giving public money away in the election campaign. Nor do you want to move the vacathio legis of this law, as we have requested, because you want its effects to be felt in 2019. And the extension of the vacathio legis would allow municipalities to prepare for this change and upgrade the rates for the use of perpetual land everywhere. We note that this update is a long-term activity and some municipalities are only in the process of these changes. You will lead to the fact that in one municipality the neighbours will have to pay very differentiated rates of the conversion fee, which will be socially unfair. I would like to remind you that since 2020 in the revenue of the Treasury, the position will disappear: perpetual usage fees, but you are not interested in what will be after 2019. You need money immediately at the expense of the future income of local government units. Every responsible government should act prospectively, thinking not only about the next election year, but about future generations. I would like to point out that on 10 March 2015, the Constitutional Tribunal decided that the request to convert the perpetual right of use into the property right of municipal land caused gross interference with the property right of the municipality. This shall be governed by Article. 165th Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which says: paragraph. 1. Local government units have legal personality. They are entitled to property rights and other property rights. Paragraph 1 is The independence of local government units is subject to judicial protection. The Court found that it had been broken in relation to municipal land as it infringed property rights. The question of the compatibility of this law with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland remains questionable. At the committee meeting on 12 July, it was reported by the Polish City Association and even by the Legal Office of the Sejm. Despite the reservations I have put forward, the Civic Platform supports the possibility of taking over the land used by Poles. [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]] At the same time, we hope that you will support our efforts to compensate the municipalities for their losses. Thank you. [[Applause]] [[Well done, Member.]]

All those who wish to use this formula will be able to benefit from the statutory institution, which is set out in the law adopted. [[And throwing it on the pavement?]] You know, it's like today we have a commercial lease and a municipal lease. This is how I visualize it. A commercial lease means a renter's advantage and less rights for him who rents it. In the case of municipal rental, there are far more rights for the tenant than for the tenant. In the case of institutional leases, we have a balance situation when the interests of both parties are equal and without this there is no development of the rent building. I would like to say more about some of the general issues that should sound in this debate. Firstly, we have really guaranteed the constitutionality of these solutions. If someone asks me where these 20 installments came from, I can say, the fact that these instalments are 20 is the price for the constitutionality of this solution. Why? Because this is how we maintain the principle of proportionality imposed on us by the Constitutional Court. This is a situation where these revenues will be at the level of the current revenue generated from the recast of the law under the Act of July 2005. This law gives nearly 2.5 million users eternal property and protects them from something that is today in the legal doctrine a dispute that can have really far-reaching consequences if it does not remain merely a theoretical dispute and will have a practical dimension. It's what happens if you end up using it forever. I wouldn't want any Pole to think about what's next. On 1 January 2019 he will have a guarantee that he will own not only his house, his apartment, but also the land on which this house is located. We've really done our homework on the Constitutional Court judgments. I wrote a whole series of quotes here, and I quoted them at the committee meeting. This sentence, which was referred to by your Member, concerned an extension to legal persons. We have avoided those charges which could be brought by the tribunal in respect of this project. As to the substance of the matter, the tribunal had no doubt, because, I quote the judgment of the tribunal: The conversion into property of perpetual use, which was established for the construction of housing, is constitutionally justified. And we have fulfilled these conditions, which are necessary to ensure that this perpetual use is in line with the constitution, namely, firstly, that we have covered the regulation of all perpetual users. Uniform feature: These are users of perpetual land built with residential buildings that meet housing needs in these buildings. Secondly, we have provided payment at the level guaranteeing landowners income such as those resulting from the existing system of conversion fees. And thirdly, we have eliminated the emergence in the future of perpetual use for housing purposes. On the other hand, I would like to say one thing to the state that defends the government today, in my opinion, without need. The Court pointed out that local government ownership is not private property but was created in the course of systemic changes on the basis of state assets. Therefore, the protection of local government property is subject to a specific modification and the municipalities must be faced with the limitation of the rights granted to them, whenever this is required by ordering a legacy which is not compatible with the new system conditions from the period of the Polish People's Republic. This is part of the ruling. Print an expression - this is absolutely what we're dealing with here in this project. This is a situation that is not compatible with today's business conditions, including housing. For what reason? Due to the fact that perpetual use was created at a time when no one isolated the premises in multi-dwelling buildings. Today, everyone who owns the apartment owns the apartment and will own the land as of 1 January 2019. Self-governments really shouldn't complain. Because we from local governments also remove the obligation to update fees, while also giving the possibility to update fees in 2018 in accordance with the Constitution. Because the basis for issuing the certificate and calculating the conversion fee will be the decision of 1 January 2019. In short, we do not restrict local authorities in any way, although I hope that local authorities in 2018 will limit themselves in updating these fees, because we have often faced such an absurd situation that after years of lack of updating, the post office suddenly brought information to the citizen, to the Pole, that from today, from 1 January of one year his fee has increased 7- or 8 times. The electronic form of this document will certainly improve the process of movement of products. The project, in line with the aim of the designers, also provides details and interpretations of the rules, removes the doubts that have arisen following organisational changes in the tax administration. Namely, after the introduction of the National Tax Administration, there were legal gaps concerning the decision-making on excise duty collection and movement of excise goods. The project eliminates these doubts and indicates the competent tax authorities e.g. to rule on the refund of excise duties on intra-Community supplies and exports of excise goods or on the basis of the applicant. The project abolishes the mandatory obligation to convoy excise goods so far. The decision to convoy will be taken by the head of the customs and treasury office, as it was before the introduction of the National Tax Administration. We also consider it a positive feature of the project to be the rules on losses in the production of beer and wine products. Here, the principle of natural losses already applied in EU legislation has been adopted and the principle of calculating interest on excise arrears has been clearly established. Of course, the project deserves particular attention from the tax preferences for natural gas, both CNG and LNG, which are intended to drive internal combustion engines. The liquefied LNG gas, mainly used in passenger cars, is already subject to tax relief. The use of preferences should be assessed very well also in the case of so-called. heavy CNG gas used e.g. in public transport. This gas is one of the most ecological motor fuels, hence its use, especially in cities, will certainly reduce the degree of smoke and smog. Let me remind you that it is already used in certain cities, regardless of the fact that it was subject to excise duty. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u170	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the Government's draft law amending the Water Law Act and certain other laws in print No 2727. The Marshal of the Sejm sent it on 5 July this year. Draft law in print No 2638 to the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation for first reading. The Commission meeting was held on 6 July. carried out the first reading and considered the same bill. High Izbo! The draft amendment of the Act resolves interpretation doubts concerning administrative procedures related to the granting of water law consents and the raising of fees for water services. The proposal also proposes to reduce the formalities related to administrative procedures relating to water law agreements and commitments. The draft law will exclude maritime authorities from the obligation to pay tolls for the exploitation of precious goods in the context of the protection of the seashore and the maintenance/deepening of the waterways. The obligation to protect the seashore and to maintain/ deepen the waterways arises from legislation and maritime authorities should not be charged for such activities. The fees for water services will not be charged if the amount fixed by the Polish Waters or the mayor, mayor or president of the city does not exceed 20 PLN. In such cases, there will be no obligation to send information on the amount of the fee to operators. The amount of PLN 20 was adopted as a threshold for cost-effectiveness of sending correspondence on information on the amount of the fee. By 31 December 2020, water users will have to make a statement to the Polish Waters on the basis of which fees for water services will be fixed. A large proportion of water-service plants measure the quantities of water and waste water entering or entering water. The solution adopted will reduce the possible interference of Polish Waters in the activities carried out by the plants and reduce the burden that would entail, among others, the burden. with water management control at the premises. The new rules will also ensure transparency of the activities of Polish Waters when calculating the fee for water services and providing information to establishments in this respect. The amendment of the Act also adds a provision which will result in the submission of one dossier, to one organizational unit of Polish Waters, in the scope of projects which simultaneously carry out activities requiring water law permits and water law applications respectively. The introduction of the provision will foster coordination of actions concerning a single investment purpose, the implementation of which currently requires the application of two separate procedures carried out by separate organisational units of Polish Waters. The amendment of the Act will also remove the obligation to publish in the local press information on the initiation of proceedings for the issue of a water permit. After the changes the proceedings will be shortened and the obligations of the State Water Farm Polskie will be reduced, which conducts many administrative procedures on water permits. And further - banning the introduction of rainwater into groundwater and banning the introduction of rainwater containing substances particularly harmful to water equipment. The amendment of the Act will allow entities that have contracts with Polish Waters to continue contracts without having to carry out another tender, will remove the obligation to have water permits in case of temporary locating, up to 180 days, construction facilities in areas particularly threatened by flooding. Amendments of an editorial and legislative nature were tabled during the work on the project. The Commission adopted without objection a report on the draft law. High Seym! On behalf of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, I request the adoption of the committee's report in print 2727. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u171	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Krzysztof Zaremba, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u172	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I wanted to present the club's position regarding the amendment of the Water Law Act. The reform of water management in Poland, which was carried out by the Sejm's adoption of the new Water Law Act of 20 July 2017, was a necessity resulting from many years of experience in the fragmentation of competences - the separation between the various organisational units of both government and local government administration - in this area. Such a system hindered the creation of a genuine water management system for the country's water resources. The creation of the National Water Farm of Poland enabled them to assume responsibility for the entire water management in Poland. This will in the future avoid the situation that occurred during the floods in 2010, when it was difficult to determine who was responsible for a given section of the flood shaft due to ongoing disputes of competence and the number of administrations that had only a section of water management competence. Two judgments of the Court of Justice of the European Union have been completed by the new Water Law. The condition for the European Commission to mobilise funding for flood-related investments has been met. At the moment, there are no infringement procedures related to the lack of transposition of European law or its inadequate transposition in the field of water management. It is worth noting that the arrears left by the previous government in this area were so great that they threatened with real financial sanctions for Poland. The analysis of the application of the Water Law Act from 1 January 2018 showed the possibility of improving the administrative formalities related to administrative procedures concerning water regulations, ensuring a more efficient system of fees for water services and facilitating the management of real estate by the Polish Waters. We have therefore proposed a number of amendments, and in particular: by 31 December 2020, water users will have to make statements to the Polish Waters on the basis of which fees for water services will be set. A large proportion of water-service plants measure the quantities of water and waste water entering water or land. The solution will reduce the possible intervention of Polish Waters in the activities carried out by the plants and reduce the burden that would involve water management control at the premises. The new rules will also ensure transparency of the activities of Polish Waters when calculating the fee for water services and providing information to establishments in this respect. The fees for water services will not be charged if its height, as determined by the Polish Waters or the Mayor, Mayor or Mayor of the City, does not exceed 20 PLN. In such cases, there will be no obligation to send information on the amount of the fee to operators. The amount of PLN 20 was adopted as a threshold for cost-effectiveness of sending correspondence on information on the amount of the fee. It was also proposed to remove the obligation to have a water permit in the case of temporary locating, up to 180 days, construction facilities in areas particularly at risk of flooding. It is also proposed to add a provision which will result in the obligation to submit a single dossier to one organizational unit of Polish Waters for projects which simultaneously carry out activities requiring appropriate water law permits, as well as to allow entities which have concluded contracts with Polish Waters to continue contracts without having to conduct a tender. Given the objective of the proposed amendment to Water Law, which is undoubtedly to reduce administrative formalities, I declare on behalf of the Law and Justice Club an amendment aimed at facilitating investment in the construction of new water equipment. The amendments also extend the time limits for making declarations by water users and clarify the remaining proposed regulations. In conclusion, the draft law contains legal solutions that will be received positively by water users, reduce bureaucratic barriers and reduce the duration of administrative proceedings. Therefore, this amendment with amendments deserves to be adopted by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u200	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I present the position of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle towards the government's draft law amending the Water Law Act and some other laws. The proposal for a procedated amendment of the Act resolves interpretation doubts concerning administrative procedures related to the granting of water law consents and the charging of water services. The proposal also proposes to reduce the formalities related to administrative procedures relating to water law agreements and commitments. The solutions recommended in the law consist of: 1. The establishment that the fees for water services will not be paid if the amount fixed by the Polish Waters or the mayor, mayor or president of the city does not exceed 20 PLN. In such cases, there will be no obligation to send information on the amount of the fee to operators. The amount of PLN 20 was adopted as a threshold for cost-effectiveness of sending correspondence on information on the amount of the fee. 2. Enable entities that have contracts with Polish Waters to continue contracts without having to tender. 3. Elimination of the obligation to have a water law permit for temporary locating, i.e. up to 180 days, construction works in areas with special flood risk. 4. Excluding maritime authorities from the obligation to pay mining fees in the context of the protection of the seashore and the maintenance/deepening of the waterways. The obligation to protect the seashore and to maintain/ deepen the waterways arises from legislation and maritime authorities should not be charged for such activities. 5. Adding a provision, which will result in the obligation to submit one dossier to one organizational unit of Polish Vodka, in the scope of projects which simultaneously involve activities requiring water law permits and water law applications. The introduction of the provision will foster coordination of actions concerning a single investment purpose, the implementation of which currently requires the application of two separate procedures carried out by separate organisational units of Polish Waters. 6. Elimination of obligations related to the publication in the local press of information on the initiation of proceedings for the issue of a water licence. After the changes the procedures will be shortened and the obligations of the State Water Farm of Poland will be reduced, which conducts many administrative procedures on water permits. 7. Prohibition of introduction of rainwater into groundwater and prohibition of introduction of rainwater containing substances which are particularly harmful to water equipment. The amendments made to the Water Law Act will benefit from administrative procedures related to the granting of water legislation and the collection of fees for water services, as well as reduced formalities related to administrative procedures relating to water law consents and promises. In view of the above, the Free and Solidarni Circle supports the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u203	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u204	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Law and Justice Club on the Government's draft law amending the Animal Identification and Registration System Act 2672 and 2733. The animal registration system is a very important issue in combating infectious animal diseases. At a time when we entered the European Union, the implementation of Union law in this area for bovine, ovine and caprine animals was one of the necessary conditions for us to be able to apply effectively for the status of a country officially free from certain infectious diseases, e.g. bovine brucellosis, bovine tuberculosis, ovine and caprine brucellosis. In recent years, in connection with, for example, ASF, these obligations also apply to pigs. The review of the existing provisions of the Act indicates the need for certain changes aimed at increasing flexibility, simplification and reduction of administrative burdens, while, of course, complying with EU rules. An important amendment is intended to address the issue of pigs at the headquarters of flocks located in the protection, surveillance or other restricted area. The rules currently in force require pig holders to notify the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture of the increase or decrease in the number of the herd and slaughter of pigs within 24 hours of the occurrence. However, due to difficulties in meeting this 24-hour deadline, it is proposed to extend it from 24 hours to 2 days. At the same time, this deadline will not apply to cases of pig births. The possibility of marking the pigs until the 30th day of birth shall be left here and, in the event of leaving the herd's seat within 30 days of the date of birth, no later than before the animals leave the herd's establishment, and a period of 2 days shall be introduced for the notification of the marking of the pigs born there, calculated from the date of the marking. Significantly, the 2-day notification deadline does not apply to cases of increase or decrease in the number of herds and slaughter of pigs prior to the designation of restricted areas. In such cases, even if during the course of the 7-day notification period the area concerned has been designated as a protection, surveillance or other restricted area, that 7-day period shall not be replaced by a 2-day period. The proposed amendments should be defined as ordering and fulfilling the obligations of pigs-holders in restricted areas. Another amendment concerns the reduction of the burden on equidae owners. This is a change at the request of the Polish Horse Breeders Association. There will be no obligation for a translator to submit a certified part of the identification document. This will reduce the cost of registration of this document. The proposed amendments will also increase flexibility in pig labelling methods - i.e. an earring or tattoo, and, in the case of an earring, any bivalve molluscs, as well as clarification and unambiguous indications on the handling of loss or damage to the earring in respect of different animal species. The obligation for pig holders to notify the Agency's manager of the additional marking of the pig is repealed. This is another good step to reduce administrative burdens. At the request of the cattle-breeding organisations, the obligation to keep an inventory of the animals in the herd and to transfer it to the agency was abolished. This meant replicating the information already collected. As regards ovine and caprine animals, it is left to make lists of ovine and caprine animals, but as a result of having a centralised database, the obligation to transmit the result of that census to the Agency is abolished. Another simplification is the exclusion of trade fair operators from the obligation to keep pig registration books, as this also implies duplication of this information. Finally, it is proposed to amend the criminal rules, which are generally beneficial for entities covered by the amended law and remove interpretation doubts. Given that the proposed changes will have a positive impact on livestock maintenance and marketing activities while ensuring the effectiveness of the tools needed to combat infectious animal diseases, the Law and Justice Club supports the proposed changes. Thank you very much.

According to the Act, instead of translations, the information referred to in Article will have to be provided. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u217	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Extraordinary Committee on Deregulation, I have the honour to present a report on the draft law amending certain laws in order to reduce the social contributions of natural persons carrying on less-scale economic activities. The primary objective of this amendment is to adjust the burden borne by entrepreneurs on social security contributions to their financial capacity. The intention of the regulation is also to: develop entrepreneurship, stimulate professional activity, reduce so-called. the grey zone and improving the survival of entrepreneurs. One of the biggest fixed costs incurred by individuals engaged in low-income economic activities is social security contributions. Under the Social Insurance Act, these include compulsory pension, pension, accident and voluntary sickness insurance. According to the rules in force, the minimum amount of social security contributions for these entities in 2018 is PLN 843.45 per month. This level of social security contributions is an important factor affecting decisions to suspend or cease business. This draft law introduces reduced and proportionate to the income of social security contributions for individuals engaged in an individual business activity and having an annual income not exceeding 30 times the minimum wage. Entrepreneurs will be able to benefit from this solution for 3 years in the last 5 years. Additional conditions include operating for a minimum of 60 days in the previous calendar year in a taxable form other than a tax card, not doing other non-agricultural activities than economic activities in the previous calendar year - creators and artists, partners of personal companies, professionals, persons running schools - and not doing business for the former employer within 2 years of its commencement. This project minimises the administrative burden imposed on entrepreneurs. They will be obliged to declare the annual revenue at the beginning of the calendar year on a one-off basis. In addition, checks on the correctness of payment of contributions will take place without the participation of the entrepreneur, electronically, in relations between the Social Insurance Institution and the National Tax Administration. It can be estimated that the law will cover more than 170 thousand. entrepreneurs. A significant socio-economic impact of the draft law will be the abolition of the disproportionate burden on low-income entrepreneurs, among others. relating to seasonal activities. Such entrepreneurs should bear adequate, lower public-law burdens, which would be in line with the spirit of the constitutional principle of social justice. There will also be a reduction in the so-called. The gray zone. Although this phenomenon is difficult to measure, according to estimates of the Central Statistical Office about 600 thousand. entrepreneurs currently operate in the gray area. This bill directly affects only micro-entrepreneurs. Its impact is to reduce the burden of compulsory social security and make them dependent on income. This will first translate into an increase in the revenue of the target group. The number of micro-entrepreneurs should be expected to increase due to improved economic survival, incentives for legalisation of the so-called so-called. informal activities in the grey economy, as well as an incentive to start up new businesses. This draft law amending certain laws to reduce the social security contributions of natural persons doing business on a smaller scale is another good change for entrepreneurs after the constitution for business and the law on the succession of natural persons doing business, which fully implements the assumptions adopted in the "Strategy for Responsible Development" in the area of small and medium-sized enterprises. No substantive amendments were tabled during the first reading. The Extraordinary Commission on Deregulation after first reading and considering this draft law at its meeting on 17 July 2018 requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. Thank you very much.

This system screams to people: you are idiots, if you have children, count on the neighbor's children. And let's not be surprised that we have a demographic disaster and collapse in Poland, because with such a pension system this demographic collapse is simply a necessity. And it would be enough to make just one small change. If in the ZUS system, according to the same rules that apply now to everyone else, make a change that only means that working children pay their contributions to their parents and only their parents, it would be such a great incentive to have children that we certainly would not have a 1.3 child per woman, but it might be two or more children, because especially those women who stay and take care of them would have a much greater pension than those who do not have children. In this direction, changes in the pension system should be made, not towards patching holes in a bag that cannot be patched. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u251	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position towards the government's draft law amending certain laws in order to reduce the social security contributions of individuals carrying out business activities on a smaller scale. For a long time there has already been a solution, which is the preferential amount of contributions to the Social Security Office in force during the initial 2-year period of operation. I am pleased that today we are already discussing this declaration as a concrete bill. The regulation contained in the novel implies that the amount of the premium base is dependent on the income generated and includes individual single-member entrepreneurs whose revenues do not exceed 2,5 times the minimum wage. The proposed mechanism will be open for 36 months in the last 60 months. I am convinced that the solutions proposed by facilitating the start-up of less profitable businesses are in line with social expectations and will have a positive impact on the development of entrepreneurship, especially in smaller towns. I also agree with the designers, according to which for many people they will also encourage them to leave the grey zone and register their activities. However, I have reservations about the mechanism for establishing the lowest basis for the assessment of contributions, which is intended to depend on the revenue generated in the previous year. This provision makes this solution not present aid in cases where the company's revenue is unexpectedly lower for several months. The privileges of the amendment under discussion today will not be available to such an entrepreneur until the following year, which, unfortunately, may no longer be any help for him to maintain his business. Despite this reservations in my opinion, the project will have a positive impact on the development opportunities of small enterprises, and therefore, near Free and Solidarnia it will vote in favour of its further process.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u255	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I would like to present my position on the draft law amending the Law on the Information System in Health Protection and on certain other laws, prints No. 2674 and 2750. The proposed changes concern the introduction of the patient's online account, in short the IKP, which is a free web application that will allow you to pass on to the patient, among others. information on health care benefits, information on reimbursement prescriptions, waiting lists for the provision, referrals, orders for medical devices, submitted declarations of choice of doctor, nurse and midwife of the POZ, held on a given day's right to health care and on the basis of that right, will allow the submission of statements of consent or withdrawal of consent to the person authorised to make medical records available, the provision of information on health status, the provision of certain health benefits, information on medical exemptions, the amount of health insurance contributions and the amount refunded in respect of medicinal products, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses or medical devices. The Act obliges the National Health Fund to provide information on the provision of health care services, the amount of public funds spent to finance these benefits and the right to health care on a given day and the basis for that right - therefore these data will be available by the IKP. In the case of surgery or the use of a treatment or diagnosis method presenting an increased risk for the patient, consent to the provision of health services, via the IKP, will take the form of a document. The patient will be able to receive information about the amount of public funds to finance the benefits provided to him both with the NFZ and with the IKP, as well as medical records. The regulations introduced will allow the patient to access some of the services offered by the Integrated Patient Informant, made available by... via the IKP. The next issue - service providers and medical registers have been obliged to ensure that their ICT systems comply with the minimum technical and functional requirements included in the Public Information Bulletin kept by the Minister for Health. The regulations introduced by the Act are intended to establish such a system of information and data management, whereby a patient with ready results will arrange for a doctor's appointment. Nurses and midwives will be able to issue prescriptions, as will doctors, not only after personal examination of the patient, but also after examination through ICT systems or communication systems. The prescription necessary to continue treatment, prescription or order for medical devices will be possible without examination of the patient if this is justified by his medical condition reflected in the medical records. The most important success of the proposed changes in the bill is that the patient will have the full information needed to obtain health benefits, he will not have to bother doctors every time he needs medicines for long-term treatment, he will learn how long he will wait for a place in the sanatorium or specialist treatment - in a word of changes in the bill and monitoring will allow every patient with much greater comfort to take care of his treatment and health. What is very important, these changes will result in benefits both for the patient and for the state. It should be stressed that the changes being examined are in line with European Union legislation. In this reading, as a club of law and justice, we propose one amendment. The Law and Justice Club welcomes this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The content and meaning of these amendments, ladies and gentlemen, are actually the same, because these amendments are talking about one thing, about the use of already existing provisions of the regulation governing the mail order sale of medicines not on prescription, because such provisions are already, also to regulate mail order sales of medicines in the case of prescription medicines sold to disabled people. This is actually the point of the amendment that you and the state have tabled. And this is a very good direction because of the doubts that have arisen today. Should all medicines be marketed by mail order? Probably not, and therefore there is a consultation of this regulation, which will take place publicly and with due respect for time limits. I understand your objections, but I would like to explain that we read it in such a way that the subject of support for people with disabilities has actually been extremely important, very often addressed in recent weeks, and probably hence such an idea that such an amendment should be introduced in such a project. I have listened to your voices and I believe that none of you had any doubts. as to the fact that the intention is good, only comments were submitted were that this should be secured. The security offers are as follows. In the regulation, we will determine what cannot be sold by mail. There's just a list of drugs you're not gonna be able to sell by mail. Secondly, we're going to determine the exact location. As you will learn about the regulation that is already in force, you will see that it is really very retail, there is even a place where packages are packed. 'Cause this is where the Amazons come in, the Ubers. Ladies and gentlemen, mail order sales of drugs were and will only be possible from the pharmacy. This has always been the case and no one is planning to change anything about it, because it is the pharmacist who knows how to ensure that the marketing of medicines is conducted in a credible and safe manner. We do not change this, and in the framework of this regulation, which, as I have already said, we intend to prepare through a very detailed consultation with the apothecary community, we will determine what conditions must be met, what must definitely not be sent, and we will introduce under another regulation, which describes the method of issuing prescriptions, a specific category identifying persons with disabilities, so that the doctor who will prescribe the medicine can indicate that this is the person who, due to his disability, may benefit from this additional entitlement. And another, very important, subject that you have raised - the issue of illegal drug exports. That is why I wanted to draw attention - the same is true in both the draft amendment - to the fact that the prescription that can be made at a distance will only be in electronic form. Ladies and gentlemen! This will allow live monitoring, in real time, when, what recipe, where and for whom it is displayed. This is the best way to fight fraud. Just not the man, not the inspector who has to go to the pharmacy today, he has to go to the warehouse, he has to go to the health care facility, no, just the IT system, who at the time of the prescription knows that the prescription has been issued, and he knows that there is a deviation, because some doctor suddenly prescribed something very much. Then we don't have to wait for the inspection to take place until we have some checks. No, ladies and gentlemen, we see it right away. And today, in the case of a non-refundable paper prescription, we see it only when the check comes. And pharmacies in Poland we have, ladies and gentlemen, 15 thousand. Although we are strengthening the Main Pharmaceutical Inspectorate, we will never strengthen it as much as it will strengthen its ability to use information systems. Of course, we want people with disabilities to be able to leave the house, to participate in social life just like any other person, and we know that this is not always possible. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u319	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Committee on Culture and Media on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Radiophony and Television and the Act on Subscription Fees. In the justification of this report we read that the project aims to bring Polish law into line with the principles of the common market. In accordance with the preliminary opinion adopted by the Directorate-General. The European Commission's competition on 10 May 2011 with regard to the compatibility of the current system of financing Polish Television SA with the common market rules, the financing of TVP through subscription fees and subsidies from the State budget constitutes State aid within the meaning of Article. 107. 1 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. The preliminary opinion stresses that this aid should be considered existing and compatible with the common market. However, as highlighted by the Directorate-General for Agriculture and Rural Development. The European Commission's competition, the system of financing public television in Poland itself, requires full consideration of all public aid rules for public broadcasters set out in the European Commission's Communication on the application of State aid rules to public service broadcasting of 27 October 2009. The preliminary opinion identifies areas where there are reasonable doubts as to whether they are fully regulated in our legal system in accordance with the Communication. Directorate-General for Agriculture and Rural Development Competition stressed the need to clarify the definition of the public service mission, to establish a procedure for prior assessment of the envisaged significant new media services, to introduce clear legislation on the limitation of public funding to the net costs of the public service mission and to supervise possible overcompensation in this respect, to regulate the principles of effective oversight of the quality of the public service mission. The draft law implements the provisions expressed in the communication to the Polish legal order in order to fill the legal gaps identified by the European Commission. At the same time, it is the implementation of the commitments of the Republic of Poland agreed with the European Commission in this respect, which were adopted by the Committee for European Affairs of the Council of Ministers on 21 September 2017. High Izbo! The Committee on Culture and Media of 21 June 2018 carried out the first reading of this draft law and appointed an emergency subcommittee to work on the Act. The subcommittee examined the bill on 3 July this year. During the work of the subcommittee, a number of legislative, linguistic, codification and substantive amendments were made to the draft. On 17 July this year, the Committee on Culture and Media examined the subcommittee's report, introducing four amendments to the report amending the legal terms. The Commission adopted the subcommittee report with one abstention. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I would like to ask the High House to adopt a proposal to amend the Act on Radio and Television and the Act on Subscription Fees attached to the report. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u320	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Puda, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u321	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Act on Radio and Television and the Act on Subscription Fees. The main objective of the revision of the Radio and Television Act and the subscription is to align the rules with the 2011 European Commission's position requiring maximum clarification of the public media mission and to reduce State aid to public media. The preliminary opinion identifies areas where there are reasonable doubts as to whether they are regulated in our legal system fully in accordance with the Communication. Directorate-General for Agriculture and Rural Development Competition stressed the need to clarify the definition of the public service mission, to establish a procedure for prior assessment of predictable relevant new media services, to introduce clear legal provisions for limiting public funding to the net costs of the public service mission and to supervise its possible compensation in this respect. The main assumptions of the proposed amendment are to clarify the definition of the public service mission of public broadcasting units and the catalogue of their mission tasks. The public service broadcasting tasks resulting from this public service mission have been complemented, inter alia. about online activities and digital technologies. Importantly, the amendment also introduces in Article. 21a a duty card specifying the mission tasks of public service broadcasters. The Charter will be the result of an agreement between the sender and the Chairman of the National Radio and Television Council. In line with the amendment, the broadcasters will set out, with the chairman of the National Council, the provisions of the Charter, which projects will be carried out on a public mission and the plan to finance them both from public funds and from commercial activities. The draft plan will be submitted to the National Radio and Television Council by the end of April, but its final version is to be agreed and published by the end of November. These plans will form the basis for the transmission by the National Council to broadcasters of broadcasting subscriptions. These measures will not be higher than the net cost of the public service mission, i.e. expenditure on projects carried out under this mission less the revenue from these projects. According to the amendment, public broadcasters will have the right to retain a maximum of 10% measures from the National Council if they bear less than the planned net costs when implementing the public service project. Larger surpluses will have to be returned to the regulator. The amendment provides, in accordance with the preliminary opinion, for the creation of a so-called procedure. prior assessment of the envisaged significant new media services. Any significant new public service after public consultation will be assessed in terms of its public value and impact on the market. An important element of the amendment is also the principle of supervision of the quality of the public service mission, among others. by clarifying the reporting obligations of broadcasters and extending the supervisory powers of broadcasters. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The bill is used to implement the provisions expressed in the Communication into the Polish legal order, filling the gaps created historically. In view of the fact that the provisions presented in this proposal align the provisions with the position of the European Commission in 2011, the Law and Justice Club will support and vote in favour of the proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We're all made up of media, public or national media, if someone wants to judge them that way. They are financed by Polish taxpayers and each of us has the right to demand their quality. The amendment of the law will not improve the situation in the media, it will only improve the real work of professionals who will be able to make good use of the funds that are transferred from the state budget, not only to practice pro-government policies, but simply to develop social needs. The Kukiz Club15 has a negative view of the proposed amendment because it does not bring about the necessary change in public media and in the implementation of public media missions.
