ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u340	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Grzegorz Adam Wozniak, the Law and Justice Club, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u341	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club regarding the draft law on printing 2670. The nature of the amendment to the Local Development Act of 20 February 2015 with the participation of the local community, briefly referred to as the RLKS Act, is determined by Community regulations, mainly by Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 December 2013. The proposed law aims to improve and improve the efficiency of the implementation system of the RLKS Act. The amendments to the proposed law result from the experience gained during the implementation of this system. The proposed changes are mainly related to Article. Points 1 and 2 of the amending act will help to resolve the concerns regarding the scope of the assessment of operations by the local action group during the process of selecting operations. This law proposes adding a new provision indicating that the call for proposals will also include the conditions for selecting operations which the local action group may specify for the selection of proposals in order to ensure the fullest consistency of operations with the LSR. At the same time, it is proposed to add a new article in this amended law. 18a, which would indicate the possibility for the local action group to define such conditions for the selection of operations. Such a solution fully resolves the question of interpretation as to the possibility for local groups to identify specific actions for a given selection of operational conditions, which are based on an analysis of the current state of implementation of the LSR and the content of the LSR itself. The conditions for selecting operations as such may be of the nature concerned, i.e. those relating to operations or entities, i.e. to the applicant. In addition, it is proposed to introduce restrictions on the possibility of making substantial modifications to the request for support in the process of supplementing this request at the evaluation stage of the voivodship board. The new provision aims to indicate a clear ban on modifications affecting the outcome of the selection of operations by the local action group and thus to ensure that any changes to the proposal do not adversely affect the implementation of the LSR, which is crucial for the local action groups. This amendment will remove the obstacle in the form of a lack of consistency between the content of the request for support submitted by the applicant to the local action group, e.g. as regards the amount of support and the amount of support determined by the local action group as a result of the selection of operations. The introduction of the provisions in question to ensure that the implementation of the RLKS system is improved and more efficient may have a positive impact on the activities of micro-entrepreneurs and small and medium-sized enterprises to which support may be granted in accordance with the amended law, in particular by introducing clear rules for the investigation of this support. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, please accept the bill presented above. Thank you very much.

The inhabitants themselves are perfectly able to identify ways of effectively developing and exploiting the potential of rural development, which can be achieved through support instruments. Over the years, we have seen dynamic changes in rural areas. We see that the village is moving strongly towards the development of non-agricultural functions. However, we still face a number of problems, such as hidden rural unemployment, low incomes of farmers, which are estimated to be 30%. lower than the income of urban residents, as well as poor development of road or social infrastructure. What the Polish village has in abundance is human capital. This potential should be exploited by increasing the social and economic activity of rural residents, and we should strengthen grassroots initiatives and support them. Therefore, Kukiz15 will support any initiative supporting local communities in their social and economic development. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u363	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The Public Finance Commission at its meeting today examined the Government's draft law on the amendment of the agricultural tax law, the Act on local taxes and charges and the Forest Tax Act of print No. 2668 and proposes the adoption of this draft law. The proposed amendment aims at ordering and harmonising the country-wide rules for taxation of land through which the infrastructure for electricity transmission, transmission, distribution of liquids, vapours, gases and telecommunications infrastructure is carried out. Until now, there have been no rules governing the direct taxation of these land. Thus, in one place, in some municipalities such land was taxed at the rate of agricultural or forestry tax, while in other municipalities on the land where the linear infrastructure I mentioned above was located, there was a much, much higher tax on real estate. And here's the difference, 90 times the tax rate per square meter. In the case of forest tax rates, this is about 0.5 gr per square metre, while in the case of business, property tax is 91 gr per square metre. There were so different interpretations, and therefore a mess, dissatisfaction, there was also the direction of court proceedings. This problem was particularly affected by areas where such infrastructure is located, belonging to the State Forests. As regards the percentage of land that was under electricity lines in the State Forests in 2017 and which was taxed at the highest rate of real estate tax, it was 5% Land, but already in 2018 this rate covered as much as 40% land under the lines. As a result of the adoption of the draft law, the rate of agricultural and forestry tax will apply, i.e. persons who own such land, State Forests or other persons, may be calm that there will be no conversion or change of classification of these land for tax purposes and charging them with such high taxes on real estate. For me, information was particularly valuable here, and I would like to stress that it should be made public that the issue is not only about State Forests, which, you know, raised this issue first - it is a well-organized institution with lawyers, etc. - but also applies to e.g. individual farmers, who we know are villagers, where many such land is occupied by electricity lines. Therefore, the owners of these parcels will not, I stress, be charged the rate of real estate tax on the same basis as has been the case in recent years: that there is an economic activity, because it runs e.g. the energy line. This law brings with it another blessing, another aspect. Since we do not allow this - the government does not allow such land to be taxed above, it can only be taxed on agricultural and forestry taxes, it also counters the possible rise in the prices of services provided by companies which use and use such infrastructure, because it is known that these prices would be a consequence of costs, including taxes paid. During the work in committee there was a problem of loss due to the income of local government units, because agricultural tax and forestry tax go there. Please note that there is no such loss because local governments do not receive such income. They wouldn't have gotten it if we allowed them to be interpreted freely or charged with property tax. The introduction of clear rules for the taxation of land through which linear infrastructure is carried out is therefore very important for taxable persons on the ground. Thanks to this amendment, both landowners and users of perpetual land will not be obliged to pay a higher tax solely because their land is driven by linear infrastructure facilities. The calculation of the highest rate of tax applicable to land occupied for economic activity was in particular detrimental to owners of forest land, agricultural land and even non-use land. This would lead to a situation in which an owner who does not carry out his own business or benefits from the establishment of a linear infrastructure would be obliged to pay a multiple higher tax on land. I would like to thank the Ministry of Energy, whose representatives took part in today's meeting of the Committee on Public Finance, as well as representatives of the Ministry of Environment and Finance for this valuable initiative, which will put these issues in order and give greater security to all owners of agricultural and forestry parcels, as well as to the companies that carry out energy lines. Thank you very much for your attention and, on behalf of the committee, I would like to ask you to continue on this project. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u365	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! There has also been a change in replacing the concept of technical belt with the concept of technological belt. This change is due to the frequent occurrence of the second term in judicial case law. Both concepts concern the same area of land. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u401	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats our position on the Report of the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Senate bill amending the Local referendum Act and the National referendum Act 2434 and 2696. The aim of the project discussed today is to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 20 April 2017 declaring the provisions of national referendum laws incompatible with the Constitution and the local referendum. The proposal introduces the possibility of reopening proceedings following the decision of the referenda court in matters relating to the dissemination of false information in statements, leaflets, etc. during the referendum campaign. In the case of the Local referendum law, if posters, passwords, leaflets, statements or other forms of propaganda and agitation contain false data and information, it may be requested by the district court for the confiscation of such materials, for a ban on their publication, for a correction order, for an apology ordered, for a participant to order the payment of up to 10 000. PLN in favour of a charity, a court order from a participant in proceedings to the applicant of the same amount as compensation. At present, the rules provide that the district court shall recognise these applications within 24 hours in non-trial proceedings. The decision of the court of referenda may also be lodged within 24 hours with the court of appeal (second instance). However, a request to reopen the procedure cannot be submitted thereafter. This provision was challenged by the Constitutional Court by judgment of 20 April 2017. The Court pointed out that in the so-called case law. referenda proceedings: very short deadlines for examining the case in the first and second instance are to ensure that a decision is given in such a time that voters can, on the one hand, read the court's findings before the day of the vote and, on the other hand, that a fair pre-election debate, free from pathology related to the dissemination of false messages, takes place for a sufficiently long period of time to form the will of voters. However, the TK noted that the simplification and shortening of proceedings must not lead to a finding that is not true, which does not correspond to the requirements of the rule of law. He pointed out that it should be possible to touch the final judgment of the court, which is seriously flawed. This applies in particular to a situation in which new evidence will appear after the judgment has been delivered, which shows that the final judgment does not correspond to reality - underlined in the explanatory memorandum. TK also noted that in the case of electoral proceedings, due to their speed, the risk of misconceptions is significantly increased. High Izbo! In the case of decisions in a special electoral procedure, the speed of proceedings is crucial. However, the speed and simplification of the procedure must not lead to a limitation of the right to raise a judgment in the event of serious (qualified) defects. The current exclusion of the possibility to bring forward judgments in the present case has infringed the constitutional right to consider the matter in fair proceedings.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u407	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the Commission's draft law amending the Press Law Act. Press law, as we remember, was adopted in 1984 and has been amended several times. It is worth recalling that the last amendment entered into force on 12 December Ub. This amendment, among others. changed the authorisation rules and deleted the Press Council rules. The proposed amendment introduced by the Committee on Petitions, with two amendments introduced at the meeting of the Committee on Culture, is now intended to bring the provisions of the Press Law into line with the current systemic reality and other laws. The amendment of the Press Law is intended primarily to extend the right to provide information to the press as regards people residing in Poland but not Polish citizens, as well as legal persons and entities without legal personality. The above amendment adapts the law to the provisions of the Constitution, i.e. to Article 4 (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013; 54 (1) (a) 1 the Constitution, which talks about freedom of expression and the acquisition and dissemination of information. The Constitution of the Republic of Poland guarantees freedom of expression for everyone. It should be noted that freedom of expression is one of the most important civil liberties and is the foundation of a democratic state. The draft law also provides for the amendment of the article. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. The essence of this amendment is to remove the provision relating to the reference to the Article. 254 of the 1969 Penal Code, which no longer exists in the Penal Code. The next amendment concerns Article. 37a. It is about adapting the article to the current legal solutions. No longer applies additional penalties, but criminal measures such as the deprivation of public rights, the prohibition of a specific position, the pursuit of a particular profession or the pursuit of a specific economic activity. As the rapporteur has already said, the draft has been prepared in response to a citizens' petition which the Committee on Petitions has found to be legitimate, as has the Committee on Culture and Communications. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will, of course, support the proposed bill. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u408	Thank you so much for putting your position on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. Mr Wojciech Król will present the speech on behalf of the Civic Platform Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u409	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club. The purpose of the proposed amendments is to adapt the provisions of the Press Law adopted in 1984 to current systemic reality and to other laws. Firstly, the project extends the catalogue of persons entitled to provide information to the press. Currently, the law provides that any citizen, in accordance with the principle of freedom of expression and the right to criticism, may provide information to the press. The purpose of this provision was to enable everyone, including employees of various institutions, ministries, local government units, to express themselves and to provide information to the media. The aim was not to prevent anyone from providing information to the media to a public institution, a citizen of the Republic of Poland. Therefore, with regard to the amendment, it might be appropriate to consider extending the catalogue in such a way as to leave the word "citizen" in its wording and to extend the catalogue by adding a phrase that, in addition to the citizens of the Republic of Poland, they can do they are also citizens of other countries and legal entities - legal persons. However, despite these reflections, changes should be regarded as beneficial and not leaving room for possible doubts as to the interpretation of the rule. It should be stressed that the question of interpretation that might prevent media from providing information, even if by nationals of other countries, would be contrary to Article. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 the European Convention on Human Rights, and hence the freedom to express opinions, have views, receive and communicate information irrespective of national borders. The processed change is beneficial, because we cannot allow non-Polish citizens, legal persons and entities without legal personality to be excluded and removed from the possibility of working with the press. It is a very big plus to include them clearly and clearly in the bodies empowered to interact with the press, because this will enable the media to have wider access to information guaranteed by a broad catalogue of entities capable of providing this information. I would like to point out that, in the present reality, freedom of opinion, of opinion, the possibility of communicating information is particularly important, as I think we all know. The other two changes are cosmetic changes. They adapt the law to the current rules of the Penal Code. Today, the law in the amended article refers to the no longer existing provision of the Penal Code. Another amendment is the amendment to the Article. 37a, which now reads: In the event of a conviction for a criminal offence committed by the publication of a press release, the court may refer as an additional penalty for the loss of the press material. The replacement of the refund by the wording of the criminal measures in view of the absence of additional penalties in the current legal order is correct and necessary and does not raise any substantive doubts. In summary, the proposed amendments are considered desirable and necessary. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u423	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position on the draft law amending the Public Procurement Law and the Law amending the Public Procurement Law and certain other laws, including amendments to the laws of 29 January 2004 and 22 June 2016. This draft amendment to the Public Procurement Law responds to the need to clarify the rules governing the electronicisation of public procurement. The main objective of the proposed amendments to the Law amending is to postpone the obligation to electronicise contracts in respect of public procurement procedures conducted by other contracting authorities than the central contracting authority for which the value of the contract is below the Union thresholds. The relevant directives of the European Union provide for mandatory electronicisation of the procurement process, but it is in the interest of smaller contracting companies and Polish companies to postpone this process in time. The Law and Justice Club sees the important benefits of the proposed regulation for small and medium-sized enterprises. It is also important that we minimise the risk of a decrease in the number of offers submitted in proceedings. Recognizing these arguments, our club fully supports the bill submitted. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u424	Thank you very much. Mr Antoni Mężyłko will present his position on behalf of the Civic Platform Club. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u425	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Madam President! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club vis-à-vis the report of the Committee on Economics and Development on a draft law amending the Public Procurement Law and the Act amending the Public Procurement Law and certain other laws. The government bill concerns the amendment of two laws: the Public Procurement Law of 2004 and the Law amending the Public Procurement Law of 2016 and regulates the electronicisation of public procurement contracts. The aim of the project is to postpone the obligation to electronicise contracts conducted by contracting entities other than the central contracting authority for which the value of the contract is below the Union thresholds. The Public Procurement Law has four amendments. Firstly, by definition of electronic means of communication, fax has been deleted by adapting this definition to EU regulations. It will therefore be the same as the definition in the 2002 Electronic Service Act. Secondly, it is clarified which statements must bear a qualified electronic signature. Thirdly, it has been clarified that when an economic operator submits a tender or part thereof in paper form, when permitted by the provisions of the Directive, it shall, under the rigour of nullity, sign it itself. Fourthly, the use of fax was authorised, by way of exception, in defence and security procurement procedures, since the provisions of the EU directives in this area do not provide for the use of electronic means of communication. All these amendments to the Public Procurement Law are precise and precise in nature, aligning Polish law with European Union directives. The Act amending the Public Procurement Law makes substantial changes to this project concerning the extension of the deadline for full electronicisation of contracts - in respect of public procurement procedures conducted by other contracting authorities than the central contracting authority, the value of which is below the Union thresholds - of 18 October 2018 at 1 January 2020. As the government informs us in the explanatory memorandum, by then there should already be a central e-Procurement Platform and integrated e-Service Portals. The proposed solution will increase the chances for contractors, in particular from the small and medium-sized enterprises sector, to be better prepared to electronicise contracts, including their qualified e-signatures, will provide better opportunities for smaller contracting parties to prepare for the implementation of electronic tools to handle public procurement procedures, increase the number of tenders submitted in proceedings and reduce the number of cancellations due to the lack of valid tenders due to the lack of qualified e-signatures on the part of smaller contractors. This is not regulated by EU rules and we have been able to move this deadline freely. On the other hand, the provisions of the EU Directives 2014/24 and 2014/25 on public procurement require Member States to communicate in public procurement procedures, the value of which is equal to or exceeds the Union thresholds, only by electronic means by 18 October 2018 at the latest. And it is in this law, in this draft law, that has been preserved together with the transitional provisions and the date of entry into force of the law. In the case of central contracting authorities, however, this obligation has been in place since 18 April 2017 and it works well. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Civic Platform Club, due to all the advantages that this amendment gives to small and medium-sized enterprises, will vote in favour of the bill in accordance with the report of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-18-sejm-67-1.u427	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the committee on the government bill amending the Public Procurement Law Act and the Act amending the Public Procurement Law and certain other laws from print No. 2744. The committee's report is print 2775. The bill as a sejm printing was made yesterday. The first reading of the project took place today at the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development. I have some doubts as to whether the law in such a hurry will be useful to the parties concerned and whether it will be a good law, all the more so since the project, as it turns out, is not subject to a process of agreement, public consultation or opinion. The project in its content concerns a very large number of entities which are obliged to apply this right. The main objective of the amendments is to clarify the rules governing the electronicisation of public procurement, which were implemented by the Law of 22 June 2016 amending the Public Procurement Law and certain other laws, and which resulted from the implementation of the Union Directive. 2014/24 EU, which provides for mandatory electronicisation of the procurement process. The main task of the project is to remove the obligation of full electronisation in relation to proceedings conducted by the so-called. small tenderers whose value of the contract is below the Union thresholds. This deadline was amended from 18 October 2018 to 1 January 2020. This regulation will certainly allow, especially to small entities, better preparation for the electronicisation of orders, including the implementation of electronic tools for handling proceedings, as well as their qualified electronic signature. With regard to detail, the draft deletes a fax as a means of communication from the definition of electronic means of communication, it clarifies which statements made in the proceedings must be accompanied by a qualified electronic signature and, as if the third element, indicate at which condition the offer or part of the offer may be submitted in paper form after it has been signed in hand. The project, as has already been said, was adopted by the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development with minor changes of an editorial nature. My Members' Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, despite some reservations about this urgency of proceding, in part justified, will support this project, whose main objective, as we know, is to postpone the deadline for full electronicisation in public procurement. Thank you very much.
